#7434. Snapchat adds more creator partnership opportunities

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The platform is testing an updated version of its Snap Creator Network, as well as new Visited By listings on its Snap Map.

Snapchat announced several new creator discovery and monetization opportunities, including an updated creator marketplace, new location markers for where creators have visited on the Snap Map, and an advanced agency partner program.

First, Snapchat is testing a refreshed version of its Snap Creator Network, which aims to make it easier for brands to find relevant creators to work with on Snapchat ad campaigns.

As shown in this screenshot, Snapchat’s updated creator discovery process uses artificial intelligence to better match brands with relevant creators.

As explained by Snapchat: “Advertisers can describe the exact creator or content they’re looking for, including audience, category, or campaign goals, and the Snap Creator Network will surface the best fit. Then, brands can explore the performance of creators, save them to shortlists, message them to establish partnerships, or even boost organic content, all in one place.”

Snapchat originally announced this creator marketplace upgrade in June, but it’s now launched the platform in open beta testing.

Snapchat has also introduced a new way for physical stores and locations to benefit from creator content, with Visited By listings on the Snap Map.

As shown in these examples, Visited By will add markers to places where creators that the user follows have filmed content. The highlighted listings will also include links to the relevant posts.

This could help brands tap into influencer recommendations, while also providing more incentive for stores to collaborate with popular creators in order to increase their presence in the Snap Map display.

Snapchat recently reported that 450 million people use Snap Map every month.

Finally, Snapchat launched an expanded Creator Agency Partner Program, which will provide agencies with additional training and support to ensure that they’re offering their clients the best guidance on creator marketing campaigns.

As per Snapchat, its updated Creator Agency Partner Program will include two tiers:

That will ensure that more agency providers are aware of the company’s evolving creator partnership efforts, and how to utilize its advanced discovery tools to guide brands towards the right creator partners.

Snapchat said the updated Creator Agency Partner Program is now in operation for agencies in the U.S., U.K. and the Middle East and North Africa.

#7433. Why Qualcomm teamed with F1’s new star in race for sports marketing ROI

The company’s consumer brand Snapdragon signed 20-year-old phenom Kimi Antonelli as a brand ambassador as it looks to connect with racing’s growing audience.

Snapdragon, the consumer brand of chipmaker Qualcomm, on Tuesday launched an anthem film around its partnership with Formula 1 driver Kimi Antonelli. The 20-year-old driver for the Mercedes-AMG Petronas team — of which the brand is also a partner — became the youngest Formula 1 World Champion ever earlier this month.

The video is part of a campaign timed to the 2026 Las Vegas Grand Prix on Nov. 21. It will run on F1 broadcasts outside the U.S. on Sky, on digital and in partnership with channels including Sports Illustrated and YouTube. Apple TV is the exclusive broadcast partner of F1 in the U.S., which prevents brands like Snapdragon from running traditional broadcast ads around the race.

“We've got other ways to bring it to life,” Qualcomm CMO Don McGuire said of the campaign. “We've got creators involved, we've got remixing involved, we have slipstreaming involved… hopefully turning it into an anthem for him that has really long legs.”

A 60-second hero video follows Antonelli during a day at a hotel. Wearing smart glasses and using a tablet, both powered by Snapdragon chips, the phenom listens to a version of the traditional Neapolitan song “Funiculì, Funiculà,” trains in the gym and kicks off a social media trend. The ad ends with a tagline, “Jamme, Jamme. To the top!,” which quotes the song and speaks to his meteoric rise in racing.

“We've been watching Kimi for quite some time, and we have always believed that he was meant for greatness and that he was going to ascend very quickly. I think we made the right bet,” McGuire said.

Snapdragon’s partnership with Antonelli builds on the brand’s deal with Mercedes-AMG Petronas, a strategic collaboration that kicked off in 2023. Qualcomm had previously partnered with the team in the 2010s, a deal that was not “ROI-driven, marketing-driven,” said McGuire, who became CMO in 2021. The new partnership looks to better connect the brand’s objectives with the growing audience of F1, both in the U.S. and globally.

“The sport more and more is not something that I can judge based on [advertising value equivalency]. We have some pretty good placements on the Mercedes cars, especially with cockpit cameras and things like that, but it's just so fleeting, from a visibility standpoint,” McGuire said.

Those issues are exacerbated by the Apple TV deal, which limits brands’ ability to measure impact during broadcasts. Qualcomm is committed to F1, Mercedes-AMG Petronas and Antonelli, especially as it seeks to bring the partnership from content and storytelling through activations and hospitality.

“We believe we're already seeing higher ROI coming out of some things that we've done in the short term,” McGuire said. “We believe in the sport, why we were there and the fundamentals.”

Outside of F1, Snapdragon is no stranger to sports. Since the start of the 2024-25 season, the brand has been the principal shirt sponsor of Manchester United. Despite the iconic soccer team’s on- and off-field issues in recent years, the deal has yielded “humongous ROI” for Snapdragon, McGuire said.

“When you're dealing with a sports brand that big, at scale, the brand can withstand the ebbs and flows of performance on the pitch because the fan base is so huge,” the executive explained. “That's the benchmark that we use [for sports partnerships] and maybe it's a little unfair because they are arguably the biggest sports brand in the world.”

Snapdragon’s latest F1 marketing comes on the heels of a holiday campaign that launched earlier this month. At the heart of “The Gift That Changes Everything” is a sweet 60-second hero spot that shows how a mom and dad make Christmas seem to come sooner. The parents use a Snapdragon-powered PC to learn how to build a "time machine" that gives their child a special Christmas morning experience.

The campaign is running across broadcast, connected TV and paid social in the U.S. with CTV and paid social in the U.K., Germany and France. Created in partnership with 72andSunny New York and 72andSunny Toronto, the effort iterates on a “Snapdragon. That’s How” creative platform that launched earlier this year. The campaign has seen strong preliminary results, McGuire reported.

The holiday creative arrives as the brand prepares for a season that could see hardware makers impacted by memory shortages, reducing Snapdragon’s total addressable market at a time when the Nvidia scandal is roiling the chip market. The brand continues to work to build awareness and affinity, regardless of short-term market conditions.

“This is about building muscle and emotion that can sustain the long-term,” McGuire said. “We feel really strongly that we had to lean into holiday as if it were any other holiday season, regardless of the macro conditions.”

#7429. Meta brings its updated Creator Dashboard to the web

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The new dashboard will replace other management tools as the primary interface for managing a creator’s in-app presence.

Creators and social media managers, get ready for Meta to redirect you toward yet another content management tool, which will be slightly different from the one that you just got used to.

Meta announced on Wednesday that its new Creator Dashboard is now active on the web, and that it will replace other management tools as the primary UI for managing a creator’s in-app presence.

Meta has been rolling out its updated Creator Dashboard on mobile devices over the past year, including its Creator Studio app. These new systems offer more artificial intelligence tools and more video creation options, aligning with broader trends.

The updated functionalities are now available on desktop as well, which should provide expanded capacity for overall management and oversight.

But it will also mean learning the specifics of another new management tool.

This sentence acts as a pretty good summary of the confusion that Meta regularly causes with changes to its content management apps: “Creator Dashboard’s revamp began last year, back when it was still called Professional Dashboard. Since then it has become Creator Dashboard, with Business Dashboard as the counterpart for businesses, so the tools in front of you as a creator are the ones that matter most.”

As per this overview, Meta has several management tools available, which also includes Meta Business Suite and the controls users can access in-stream. Meta keeps updating these systems to ensure that its management tools incorporate the latest functionality, in line with its evolving systems.

But understanding which platform creators and social media managers are supposed to be using can get confusing.

Meta said the updated web composer in Creator Dashboard has been redesigned for feed posts and Reels, with full-screen overviews and live previews, so creators can see what their post will look like before publishing.

There’s also bulk video upload options, an improved content library and more insights into content performance.

Content protection is also available in the new app. Meta's content protection process enables creators to register their original uploads, which Meta’s system can then use as a reference point to detect when others re-post the same thing. Meta expanded content protection to Instagram earlier this week.

Meta said that creators will also now be able to invite people who’ve checked out their content to become followers from the Creator Dashboard, and access Messenger on the web. There’s also a monetization section, where creators will be able to access relevant information about potential monetization opportunities.

So, more Facebook and Instagram management and creation tools, integrated within a single app. For now, at least, till Meta comes out with the next iteration of its management platform and forces everyone to learn an all-new system once again.

#7428. Maker’s Mark spotlights signature red wax top in visual refresh

The brand evokes its heritage and craftsmanship with a new color palette, website and typography.

Maker’s Mark, a Suntory Global Spirits brand, pulled back the curtain on a new global brand identity this week that updates the brand’s color palette, photography and graphic elements with a more minimal, modern aesthetic that evokes the brand’s heritage. It is live in the U.S., China, Australia and the U.K., with other markets planned throughout 2027, according to a press release.

The new visual identity spotlights the bourbon’s iconic red wax. Custom typography is inspired by the handwriting of Margie Samuels, one of the co-founders of Maker’s Mark, who died in 1985 and was responsible for creating the brand name as well as its original bottle design. The bottle design remains unchanged, per press materials. Tactile elements included in the wax seal, such as wheat stalks and oak rings, were created using materials found at Star Hill Farm, one of the properties owned by the brand and the namesake of its Star Hill Farm Whisky.

"In a world increasingly driven by speed and automation, we still believe some things are worth making slowly, by hand," said Chloe Lloyd-Jones, vice president of global brand marketing at Maker's Mark, in press materials.

The brand last made changes to its visual identity in 2021, but they were not as extensive. The latest refresh was developed in partnership with Turner Duckworth London and is part of the “Perfectly Unreasonable” campaign.

The new design will appear across digital, social and retail channels, along with a new website. The overhaul is supported by in-person experiences in the U.S. and China, along with out-of-home activations in Australia and the U.K.

The refresh comes as the bourbon industry faces significant challenges, including slowing demand and a bloated inventory, which are driving down prices. Tariffs have also taken a toll on the industry, with exports of American whiskey, which includes bourbon, falling 19% in 2025. The industry’s issues are exacerbated by the general trend of Americans drinking less overall. A little over half of Americans said they drink, according to a recent Gallup poll, the lowest it’s been since the organization started measuring the trend in 1939.

Suntory Global Spirits ceased production at its flagship Jim Beam distillery in Clermont, Kentucky at the beginning of 2026. It recently announced it would continue to pause distilling operations until at least the end of next year. Maker’s Mark is distilled at a different location, which remains operational.

“Perfectly Unreasonable” launched in 2025 and is the first campaign for the brand by Carmichael Lynch. In December, Maker’s Mark entered into its first sports league sponsorship and became the first official spirits partner of the women’s basketball league Unrivaled.

Suntory Beverage & Food Limited, which is traded on the Tokyo Stock Exchange, saw its revenue increase 9.8% year-over-year for the nine months ending Sept. 30, 2026, according to financial results.

#7426. Customer Growth Manager at Uscreen

Uscreen is the video monetization platform for businesses building recurring revenue from video content. Our customers replace a patchwork stack of separate hosting, community, courses, email, and app vendors with one branded experience across web, mobile, and TV.

The platform is only half of it. We believe growth requires behavior, not just software. Customers don't stall because a feature is missing. They stall because they priced wrong, never built an email list, or launched an offer nobody wanted. Coaching is how we fix that, and it's part of the product rather than an add-on.

You own a segment, and you run it like a growth marketer.

We have a cohort of smaller video businesses where retention is not where it should be. Nobody owns it today. You will. The revenue that stays in that segment, and the revenue that grows inside it, is yours.

This is a coaching role for someone with a growth marketer's mindset. We wouldn't hire a demand gen marketer who couldn't analyze the state of the business and figure out what's needed, and we won't hire a coach who can't either. You'll look at a customer's business the way an owner would: where the revenue comes from, where it leaks, and what to do next. You pull your own numbers to get there. When a customer needs a custom report to answer a growth question, you build it yourself.

It's a one-to-many job. Your cohort is larger than a 1:1 book, so the work is designing coaching that scales: cohort programs, group sessions, office hours, and triggered outreach when the data says a business is drifting. You'll build that motion rather than inherit it.

Success is measured in dollars: gross revenue retention in your cohort first, expansion second. Not tickets closed, not calls booked.

A coach with a growth marketer's mindset. Maybe you've run growth or demand gen for a small business, built your own audience, or coached founders through their numbers. You understand how a membership business makes money, you pull your own analytics to see what's working, and you're comfortable doing it in front of a client. What matters is that you can find the real constraint in a business, then sit with the operator and say the honest thing.

Benefits may vary depending on location and local regulations. Final eligibility and coverage details will be shared during the hiring process.

#7424. TikTok releases 2026 Music Impact Report

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The analysis found that 94% of songs on the Billboard Global 200 in 2025 had a viral moment on TikTok before climbing the chart.

TikTok has published its 2026 Music Impact Report, which looks at the role that the platform plays in broader music and artist discovery.

The report is based on a study conducted by Luminate, in partnership with TikTok, which incorporates responses from more than 4,000 U.S. TikTok users. The response data underlines just how influential TikTok has become as a music and artist discovery tool, with 45% of U.S. TikTok users saying they discover new music in the app. That, according to the report, is higher than on YouTube, Instagram or Facebook.

The most significant claim of the report, however, is that 94% of songs that entered the Billboard Global 200 in 2025 went viral on TikTok before climbing the chart.

That’s a significant correlation, which is up from 84% 2024, per TikTok’s 2025 Music Impact report.

The data underlines TikTok’s value as a music discovery platform, which makes it a critically important consideration for musicians and publishers when it comes to mapping out their promotional approach.

The data underlines the platform’s growing role as a music discovery tool. In some ways, TikTok has replaced the MTV of old as a key link between music trends and audiences.

That makes it a more valuable consideration for music-related marketers, while the insights also highlight the broader role that the platform plays in informing broader cultural shifts.

Which could be an important consideration for brands seeking to align their campaigns with what’s trending.

#7423. Paid media now plays largest role in brand-funded creator growth: report

While pumping paid dollars into creator content is the biggest piece of the category pie, using creators for production is the fastest growing, Madison & Wall found.

Madison & Wall’s research illustrates how creator marketing is evolving further away from the narrow #sponsored post model — what the advisory and consulting firm calls audience-led spend — into a multifaceted media engine that is core to consumer outreach. The advertiser-funded creator economy is forecast to grow 15% this year to $116 billion and reach $178 billion by 2030. It is on pace to grow at twice the rate of the total ad market as brands try to meet demands around creative content volume, authenticity and audience access.

“[Creators] have become an essential layer of the commercial advertising and media supply chain, serving simultaneously as media channels, creative production houses, commerce drivers, and performance engines,” the report reads.

Putting paid media dollars behind existing creator content that performs well has become the largest aspect of the advertiser-funded creator economy, providing reach and scalability without the TV commercial gloss that many consumers have begun to reject. Reliably achieving organic reach has also become less certain as platform algorithms change. Due to these factors, media-led creator spending is expected to reach $83 billion by 2030.

Marketers are not broadly expanding their budgets to support creator initiatives, but rather siphoning off resources previously allocated to channels like traditional media, digital search, paid social and programmatic. Madison & Wall emphasized that creator partnerships often employ multiple approaches, including combining audience-led and media-led spend, rather than focusing on one bucket. The study did not analyze consumer-funded creator income, meaning subscriptions, tips or other forms of revenue creators receive from their audiences.

While media-led spending is the biggest piece of the creator pie at the moment, asset-led spending, where brands pay creators to produce content, effectively using them as a content studio, is the fastest growing, currently representing 12% of the market. That helps explain the budgets being drawn from traditional creative production and could be a detriment to third-party creative agencies and production shops, which are also contending with the rise of artificial intelligence-generated content.

Those marketing services intermediaries, as well as talent managers and technology platforms, are retaining about 8% of advertiser-funded creator investments on a net basis as part of what Madison & Wall terms infrastructure spend. The rest of the market is made up of a miscellaneous segment that accounts for 7% of spend and encompasses creator engagements such as live events, barter arrangements and name, image and likeness deals.

The second part of Madison & Wall’s study will break down some of the growing pains the creator economy faces as it looks to sustain momentum, including fragmentation, pricing inconsistencies, contractual logjams, measurement shortcomings and questions of ownership over areas like data and intellectual property. That installment will also dive into creator fatigue, a mounting problem for marketers who have valued creators as a more approachable and trustworthy way to catch consumer attention.

Get the free daily newsletter read by industry experts

The sense of a stable middle ground is eroding across an industry at a point of contraction, with many displaying mixed feelings about marketing’s future.

Ten-figure acquisitions, new agency experiments and organizational changes are underway, but the real key may be relinquishing a sense of control.

#7419. Instagram adds Stories editing

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The platform rolled out various updates for the format, including the much-requested option to make changes to Stories after posting.

Instagram has rolled out a range of updates for Stories, including a much-requested editing option, which will enable users to update their posts after publishing.

As shown in this image, Instagram users will now be able to edit published Stories for up to 15 minutes after posting. Which isn't a huge window of time, but it should be enough to catch and correct any typos or mistakes.

It’s a significant update, which will be particularly beneficial for social media managers, enabling them to revise their content instead of having to start over again.

Instagram has also made it easier to select and post multiple images in Stories and added more display options.

It's also launched an updated sticker selector with new stickers, as well as expanded color gradient options for photo backgrounds in Story frames.

The updates will provide more ways for users to share their experiences in Stories during the upcoming holiday season, while as noted, they'll also help marketers use Stories to promote their holiday offers.

On another front, Instagram added the capacity to search saved items in the iOS version of the app.

As shown in these images, posted by app researcher Radu Oncescu, Instagram users can now search within their saved items. It’s a minor functional addition, but it could be a big help in finding previously saved posts.

#7418. How Disney helps brands interact and transact with holiday audiences

The entertainment giant’s holiday ad sales are pacing 20% ahead of the same point last year, with demand across the retail, financial services, CPG and technology sectors.

The 60th annual Country Music Association Awards, which aired last night on ABC and Disney+ and will stream tomorrow on Hulu, showcased Disney’s evolving advertising strategy in action. Chevrolet debuted new custom creative, partnered with musician and Chevy truck owner Zach Top for several in-show moments and put out social-first, behind-the-scenes content.

In addition, Bath & Body Works, a new partner to the awards show, was the first brand to be a presenting sponsor of a live integration on TikTok that captured behind-the-scenes content and was hosted by musician Lauren Alaina and creator Haley Baylee. Financial services company Ally made its CMA Awards debut, activating on in-show, social, streaming and linear channels, including content with personality Kristin Cavallari.

“We're looking at new ways beyond your traditional 30-second ad in order to be able to engage fans as well as drive new revenue opportunities,” said John Campbell, senior vice president for streaming, entertainment and multicultural solutions at Disney.

The CMA Awards arrived with just a week until Thanksgiving. The holiday period is in full swing, with the National Retail Federation forecasting holiday retail sales of more than $1 trillion. With a portfolio that spans film, TV, streaming, theme parks and consumer products, Disney reaches 54% of holiday gift-givers monthly, per the company’s research, providing advertisers with a large audience across channels.

For Disney, advertising bookings in streaming and linear across Halloween, Thanksgiving and the holidays overall are pacing 20% ahead of the same point last year, with demand across categories including retail, financial services, CPG and technology, the company said.

The holidays are now a yearlong concern, with Disney selling its first holiday deal in January and more than 10% of its holiday revenue coming in since Nov. 1. The company also saw 10% year-over-year advertising revenue growth in Q4 of its fiscal year 2026 for a total of $1.69 billion. Across the advertising landscape, holiday ads are starting as early as September, per Nielsen.

“The market is still very active,” said Campbell. “Not only are [brands] starting way earlier to build out these larger programs, but they're also coming in as we get closer to the holiday season, and we're seeing total investments grow, as well.”

Brands flock to Disney for three reasons, Campbell said. The company is a trusted source for storytelling with titles that span generations, from “Home Alone” to “The Nightmare Before Christmas;” it delivers live cultural moments, including holiday TV specials, that serve as shared experiences; and it gives advertisers multiple ways to reach consumers across platforms.

Disney’s multichannel approach relies on a StorySync strategy that works with brands to develop seven key elements: sponsorships, in-show integrations, custom content, social and influencer, commerce, experiential and halo enhancements. The company is working to add new shoppable activations to the StorySync framework that can help drive brand revenue during the holiday period.

“We not only encourage people to participate and interact with these brands, but also give them the levers in order to be able to transact with these brands through our shoppable moments,” Campbell said. “How do we add value for our consumers and our viewers, and how do we bring brands to be able to help us add that value?”

“Dancing with the Stars,” now in its 35th season, continues to be a durable property for Disney, with the competition show seeing an 11% episode-to-episode viewership increase en route to the season finale on Nov. 24, Campbell said. Sephora served as the seasonlong beauty sponsor for the show, partnering on social-first custom content and becoming the first show partner to incorporate Disney’s Shop the Stream solution.

The show will keep the action going with a holiday special on Dec. 1. Ocean Spray will have a sponsored dance, custom content, in-show mentions and lower-third graphics, while Christmas retailer Balsam Hill provided decorations and will appear in lower-third graphics.

“That franchise has expanded now,” Campbell said. “No matter the opportunity, we're seeing brands really want to attach themselves.”

In addition, Kraft Mac and Cheese is partnering with Freeform’s “25 Days of Christmas” as part of a long-term strategic partnership between Kraft Heinz and Disney that kicked off this summer. Capital One will join as the first presenting broadcast sponsor for the Rock & Roll Hall of Fame Induction Ceremony on Dec. 8, and Disney has secured new and returning sponsors for the “Wonderful World of Disney: Holiday Spectacular” and New Year’s Rockin’ Eve programming.

After the holiday season, Disney Advertising will continue apace, with the college football playoffs and national championship game, the Grammy and Oscar ceremonies and the Super Bowl, together making up what Disney ad chief Rita Ferro has described as the company’s “biggest year ever.” Next month, the company will begin introducing creator content to Disney+ through a partnership with TikTok that was announced earlier this year.

“We're very excited about some new content formats that streaming is going to be able to provide,” Campbell said, citing microdramas as a possibility.

#7414. LinkedIn study explores B2B buying decisions

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The research highlights the importance of relational factors, such as testimonials and peer validation, when it comes to influencing client purchases.

LinkedIn has published new research which examines what drives B2B buying decisions, and provides guidance on how brands can ensure that their products are as appealing as possible to prospective clients.

The study, conducted in conjunction with WARC and Lions Advisory, analyzes the factors that go into creating "buyability." The authors examined 700 B2B case studies in order to discover the crucial factors that influence purchase decisions across these examples.

The findings show that many of the key factors that drive decision-making are relational, as opposed to being grounded in product functionality.

As explained by Mimi Turner, global head of thought leadership strategy at LinkedIn’s B2B Institute: “Confidence in product capability is still very important. But once baseline requirements are met, the top priority is no longer marginal product superiority or price. It becomes whether the decision can be successfully defended even if outcomes go wrong.”

In order to test this, the study measured each of the 700 campaigns against various criteria related to buying signals.

The researchers then segmented the campaigns into "high buyability" campaigns, which were those that included three or more of these signals, and "low buyability" initiatives, which included zero, one or two signals.

The results shoed that high buyability campaigns — meaning those with more focus on testimonials, peer validation and measures of industry leadership — significantly outperformed low buyability campaigns across commercial and brand measures.

“Evaluated against the WARC B2B Effectiveness Ladder, High Buyability campaigns were also 1.7x more likely to be classified as ‘Strategic Assets,’ the highest rung of effectiveness, reserved for initiatives that drive sustained commercial growth,” Turner said.

The data also found that customer-centric insights were present in 83% of high buyability campaigns, which were also four times more likely to feature customer testimonials.

In other words, including more elements that reinforce buying decisions through relatable examples and external validation can have a major impact on reassuring buyers and securing more deals.

LinkedIn further tested these concepts in a campaign for ServiceNow, which specifically focused on these buyability elements.

“The results included a 10x increase in consideration, a 13x increase in unaided brand awareness over three years, and subscription revenue up by 23% over the same period,” Turner said.

Turner said these findings indicate that recommendations, relationships and relatable buying situations can have a significant influence on campaign outcomes.

These notes could help B2B marketers improve the resonance of their campaigns.

#7411. How SharkNinja is doubling down on its creator strategy

The home appliance brand introduced two new creator programs, SharkNinja First Fans and SharkNinja Storytellers, to tap into the quickly growing influencer economy.

SharkNinja CEO Mark Barrocas in 2008 told a retailer that his plan was to build the company “one five-star review at a time.” In Barrocas’ retelling, the retailer’s executives dismissed the idea as foolish because they couldn’t imagine who would write a review of the brand’s products. That was before the rise of influencers.

Over the ensuing 28 years, SharkNinja has become a multibillion-dollar global company, which built its brand through a social-forward, ultra-connected strategy that includes partnerships with celebrities including David Beckham, Courteney Cox and Alix Earle, alongside a host of influencers.

SharkNinja is doubling down on that strategy with two new programs designed to give storytellers, both inside and outside of the company, access and opportunities to develop and create even more content.

The programs, SharkNinja First Fans, for company employees, and SharkNinja Storytellers, for general consumers and creators, are designed to help the company continue to grow and manage its expanding list of creators and partners.

“We felt we were underserving this community by not creating a space where we could have more scaled one-to-ones with them, bring them further into the fold in terms of our product board maps, and incentivize participation,” said Michelle Crossan-Matos, chief brand and experience officer at SharkNinja.

Over 200,000 posts about SharkNinja’s varied household products, which include blenders, vacuums and ice-cream makers, have been made this year, garnering more than 19 billion impressions, according to the company. Last year, it recorded more than $6.4 billion in sales.

The secret sauce is the company’s practice of fostering, nurturing and actively supporting those who want to create content around SharkNinja’s products, according to Crossan-Matos.

“We bring creators all the way upstream with our products, and we’re having them react to and test and make content with some of our products before we go to market. It’s always been core for our strategy,” said Crossan-Matos.

The new programs use leaderboards and rewards to ensure the content keeps coming. For outside creators, the rewards, which can include commissions and perks, are tied to growth and activity. The most active creators will get exclusive first looks at upcoming products and even get a voice in shaping them. The company has created tutorials and other materials, including an artificial intelligence-powered app, to help the internal creators develop compelling narratives.

Though not exactly commonplace, employer-supported creator programs are not unheard of either. Starbucks has its Green Apron Creators, IBM has a Buzz Squad, Gap opened up its creator ecosystem to employees, and Dell has compensated its employees for sharing content on their own channels. The scope of SharkNinja’s creator efforts are notable.

“I don't know if anybody is doing it the exact way that we are,” Crossan-Matos said. “I think to have both [consumer and employee programs] launch as quickly as we did and at this scale … Our appetite is to drive this globally.”

The company can support these programs on a global scale because of its broad product base, Crossan-Matos said. SharkNinja creates products across 41 consumer categories and launches an average of 25 new products every year.

“We're very lucky to have so many products for so many different types of communities globally,” Crossan-Matos said. “Creators just fit so seamlessly into our co-creation and our storytelling. We think [these initiatives] are going to propel more people to tell their own stories in a way that is even more relatable because they're everyday people.”

Keeping an eye on metrics will be key to measuring program success.

“We’ll be looking at content performance, just like we do with everything. We will be looking for what posts are going viral, what things are gaining traction,” Crossan-Matos said. “We expect really high numbers as we expand.”

#7410. Reddit publishes guide to Super Bowl engagement trends

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Brands looking to engage with subreddit communities should begin well ahead of the 2027 game in order to map out effective approaches, per the platform.

Reddit published a new report which examines key NFL Super Bowl engagement stats and opportunities, and provides tips on how brands can tap into Super Bowl hype to maximize ad approaches.

The 12-page guide includes notes on Super Bowl and NFL engagement on Reddit, with overviews of how fans engage with the game across a range of related subreddit communities.

The guide also provides notes on how brands can build their Reddit presence, and get a handle on key trends in order to better target their approaches.

Reddit’s advice for brands essentially centers on choosing a specific content approach for their Super Bowl campaign, then honing in on the related questions will drive the most engagement.

Reddit said that brands should consider what feelings they want to evoke with their Super Bowl campaigns, and what rituals or language they want to use in order to relate to niche communities. Within this, brands should also consider how they can tell an authentic, meaningful story.

These elements link back to how Redditors engage, and by mapping out a strategic approach early that aligns with this, Reddit said that brands will be able to construct a more effective approach ahead of the Super Bowl hype in 2027.

Reddit’s guide includes a range of more specific notes on how to do this, and it could be a valuable planning tool for brands that plan to use the platform for their marketing efforts.

#7408. Senior Product Designer, Developer Platform at LiveKit

LiveKit is building the infrastructure layer for the voice-driven era of computing. Our platform gives developers everything they need to build, test, deploy, scale, and observe agents in production. Founded in 2021, LiveKit powers voice AI applications for OpenAI, xAI, Salesforce, Coursera, Spotify, and thousands of others, collectively facilitating billions of calls each year.

Most product designers want to design for everyone. This role is for someone who'd rather design for one very specific, very opinionated person: the developer at 2am trying to figure out why their voice agent just failed a call. You'll design the tools developers use to test, evaluate and debug AI agents — a category that's still being invented, with no established patterns to copy. If dense data, real-time systems and reading a stack trace sound more fun than intimidating, keep reading.

You'll thrive as a Senior Product Designer if you:

are obsessed with making the best product for our customers

are known as the go-to person for tackling tough problems

are focused on polish, detail and quality

are a fast learner, frequently picking up new tips, tricks, and skills

The best way to impress us is with thoughtful ways you'd implement LiveKit, and potentially tinkering with it 😊

Own the design of how developers test, evaluate and observe their agents — simulations, evals, logs and agent insights

Turn complex, real-time data into interfaces that are easy to scan, understand and act on

Design new interaction patterns for AI-native workflows, where agents generate the first draft and humans adjust it

Prototype in code and work hand-in-hand with engineers to ship polished work

Contribute patterns to our design system for dense, debugging-oriented surfaces

You've shipped design work for developer tools, observability or data-heavy products

You have strong data-visualization instincts and know when a chart beats a table (and when it doesn't)

You're comfortable in code — reading PRs, tweaking Tailwind, building prototypes

You earn the trust of deeply technical users and engineers

Experience designing for AI or agent tooling

AI tools are already a core part of how you design

An opportunity to build something truly impactful to the world

Contribute to open source alongside world-class engineers

LiveKit is an equal opportunity employer and does not discriminate on the basis of any characteristic protected by applicable law. If you require a reasonable accommodation during the application or interview process, please contact recruiting@livekit.io.

#7407. Samsara: Account Executive, Mid Market (West)

Samsara (NYSE: IOT) is the pioneer of the Connected Operations™ Cloud, which is a platform that enables organizations that depend on physical operations to harness Internet of Things (IoT) data to develop actionable insights and improve their operations. At Samsara, we are helping improve the safety, efficiency and sustainability of the physical operations that power our global economy. Representing more than 40% of global GDP, these industries are the infrastructure of our planet, including agriculture, construction, field services, transportation, and manufacturing — and we are excited to help digitally transform their operations at scale.

Working at Samsara means you’ll help define the future of physical operations and be on a team that’s shaping an exciting array of product solutions, including Video-Based Safety, Vehicle Telematics, Apps and Driver Workflows, and Equipment Monitoring. As part of a recently public company, you’ll have the autonomy and support to make an impact as we build for the long term.

This is a dynamic, high energy role in which you will bring the Internet of Things to mid-sized customers, building Samsara's business and bringing the benefits of sensor data to customers. Typical sales will be $20k to $100k, and involve POCs, multiple stakeholders, managing trials, multi-faceted pricing negotiations, and selling to executives and CXOs.

The compensation range shown spans approved pay zones for this role. Your primary work location determines the range applicable to you, which may be narrower than the full range displayed.

Annual on-target earnings (OTE) range for full-time employees for this position is below and depends on your city of residence. Learn more about our total rewards and benefits below.

At Samsara, we build for the people who keep the global economy moving. We want owners, not passengers, which is why our rewards are designed to fuel high-impact builders. Our compensation program delivers above-market total compensation through a combination of base salary, performance-based bonus/variable pay, and equity (for eligible roles) in a high-growth public company. We meaningfully differentiate pay for our top performers, who have the opportunity to earn above-market compensation that can outpace the broader market over time.

Beyond compensation, we provide the foundations that enable long-term success: a flexible, employee-led remote model, a professional development stipend, comprehensive health and parental leave plans, and more. If you’re ready to build for the long term and own the outcome, your journey starts here.

At Samsara, we embrace a flexible working model that caters to the diverse needs of our teams. Our offices are open for those who prefer to work in-person and we also support remote work where it aligns with our operational requirements. For certain positions, being close to one of our offices or within a specific geographic area is important to facilitate collaboration, access to resources, or alignment with our service regions. In these cases, the job description will clearly indicate any working location requirements. Our goal is to ensure that all members of our team can contribute effectively, whether they are working on-site, in a hybrid model, or fully remotely. All offers of employment are contingent upon an individual’s ability to secure and maintain the legal right to work at the company and in the specified work location, if applicable.

At Samsara, we welcome everyone regardless of their background. All qualified applicants will receive consideration for employment without regard to race, color, religion, national origin, sex, gender, gender identity, sexual orientation, protected veteran status, disability, age, and other characteristics protected by law. We depend on the unique approaches of our team members to help us solve complex problems and want to ensure that Samsara is a place where people from all backgrounds can make an impact.

#7406. Bumble cozies up to Alix Earle in attempt to convert Gen Zers

The influencer, who has previously stated her dislike of dating apps, appears in a two-minute video alongside Pete Davidson to encourage Gen Z users to give the platform a try.

Bumble is looking to appeal to Gen Z users as the demographic continues to ditch apps for real-life alternatives. User retention continues to be a problem for the dating app, which saw the total number of paying users decline from 2.5 million in Q2 2025 to 2.1 million in Q2 2026, according to an earnings statement.

The casting choices for the campaign are interesting ones as Earle has routinely rejected dating apps and Davidson is known for his string of ill-fated relationships with high-profile individuals, including a short-lived engagement to pop star Ariana Grande.

The video is self-aware regarding the history of its stars. For example, when Davidson plays an inconsiderate first date, Earle asks what Davidson does for work. He responds "Nothing much. I just kind of talk to camera.” Earle responds that she does something similar, to which Davidson replies, “yeah, but when I do it, it’s different,” a reference to controversial comments the comedian made regarding large influencer salaries and the amount of work involved.

Earle’s character is similarly aware of her past with dating apps. When confronted with the fact that she has publicly stated she hates dating apps, she responds: “I have said that publicly at least five different times on three different platforms. But sometimes I can be a little dramatic.”

Over the course of the sexual innuendo-laden spot, Earle convinces her fellow Gen Zers to try Bumble, showing off features such as searching for specific people. The women are impressed by the quality of men they find on the site, with one participant being impressed they all have bedframes.

The campaign was created in partnership with OBB Bolded and is supported by Bumble data that found 81% of singles between the ages of 18 and 25 are most interested in a relationship that adds value to the life they’re building.

Bumble has worked to attract more users to its app in recent months, including allowing anyone in a match to send the first message, a deviation away from the previous model where women had to send the first message, which was once a selling point for the brand. It also expanded the response window from 24 to 72 hours and introduced handles, a tool that allows verified users to more easily find one another on the app.

This isn’t the first time Bumble has turned to influencer marketing. In June, the app launched “Bee Line,” a talk show hosted by comedian and influencer Jake Shane that tackles dating anxiety.

#7404. Threads tests new Q&A option in live chats

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The format would allow chat hosts to solicit questions and have chat participants vote on what to ask.

Threads is testing a new Q&A option in live chats as it looks for more ways to drive active engagement and interaction among like-interested users.

As shown in these examples, Threads is experimenting with a custom format for live chat Q&A sessions, which would enable chat hosts to gather questions from participants. The proposed questions then get put to a vote in the chat, leading to a more structured interview approach.

That this could ensure more relevant questions and enhance the value of live conversations.

In some ways, it’s similar to the approach Reddit takes with its AMA sessions, where users can upvote questions in order to give them more priority, enabling the community to guide the discussion.

This move could also make live chats on Threads a more viable option for brand promotion, with brands able to participate in curated chats and answer audience questions.

Threads added live chat in April as part of its expanded effort to drive more discussion around live events in the app.

It added more live chat functionality in September, with additional tools to encourage user contribution.

Upvoted Q&A sessions are another step in that direction, driving more community engagement, and more focus in live discussion sessions.

Which, ideally, will help to make Threads a bigger focus for real-time engagement during major events. X is arguably still the go-to platform on this front, but Threads is making inroads and could soon become a bigger focus for more communities.

Threads said it’s testing the new Q&A option with a selection of live chat sessions, but all participants can read live chat responses.

#7403. Product Designer at Clerk

Clerk is on a mission to solve user management once and for all. We are a globally distributed team dedicated to providing best-in-class developer experience, with obsessive attention to every detail. Today, we provide developers with full-stack React components and hooks like , , , useUser, and useOrganization. These APIs allow developers to build hard-to-get-right user and organization management flows. We believe that a component is worth a thousand APIs.

Our Dashboard is a primary tool for configuring our components, as well as a centralized place for managing your app’s users.

You’ll join Visual Designers, UI Engineers, and fellow Product Designers on the Clerk Design team to build our core product, the Clerk Dashboard. You’ll always keep developer experience first as you gain a deeper understanding of the business needs of user management. You’ll optimize for customer delight, flexibility, and ease-of-use within a complicated, technical domain.

Collaborate with teammates to design new features. You’ll work closely with Product, Design, and Engineering across project lifecycles from ideation to implementation.

Gather requirements. You’ll research the technology and customer impetus behind the features we release. You’ll attain a deep understanding of the problems we solve.

Structure and prototype. You’ll create flows and interactions that make sense of complex information, whether it’s a static component or a multi-page view.

Not settle for less than pixel-perfection. You’ll work with a world-class UI Engineering team to bring your Figma frames to life. You set the bar for quality.

Elevate our design culture. You’ll support and expand the Clerk Design visual language, component library, accessibility practices, design system, and design processes.

You’re a Senior(+) level Product Designer with experience working in authentication, user management, or developer tools. You love your craft. You push Engineers to build experiences they’re unsure if they’re capable of (and you expect them to do the same for you).

You’re in the Americas. The Clerk Design team is spread across the globe; Product Design currently spans UTC+1 to UTC+5:30. You live in a location that is ±3 hours within U.S. Mountain Time (MDT, UTC−06:00).

You have technical fluency. You have a deep understanding of designing interactive software on the web. You don’t need to author production-ready front-end code, but it’s a big plus if you can.

You work iteratively. Your process embodies a “ship and iterate” mentality. You value the quick pace of a growing startup like Clerk.

You own your time. You’re a self-starter who would rather find a tough problem to solve instead of waiting for a new task. To be successful at Clerk, you need to make the most of the autonomy you’re given.

💰 Competitive Salary – We want you to know that we value the skills and experience you bring to the table. We go out of our way to make sure that you feel fairly compensated.

📈 Equity Ownership – At Clerk, we believe in shared success. That's why we offer a stock option plan so that everyone can benefit from the growth and prosperity of the company.

⚕️ Health Coverage – We care about your well-being. That's why we offer top-tier health insurance to ensure that your health needs are fully met.

🎧 Work Gear - Set up your ideal home office with the gear of your choice. At Clerk, we want to ensure that you have everything you need to perform at your best.

🏖️ Flexible Vacation Policy – We believe in work-life balance and trust you to take the time you need. Although we recommend 25 days per year, our vacation policy is unlimited. This is in addition to observing national holidays specific to your country of residence.

🌍 Diverse and Inclusive Team – Join our exceptional, diverse, and globally distributed team at Clerk. We are committed to fostering an inclusive environment where everyone can contribute their best in building impactful products and tools for the modern web.

#7401. Sociable: Instagram adds Amazon affiliate links

The partnership will enable creators to add up to 30 shoppable products per Reel or 20 per feed post.

“Sociable” is the latest commentary on important social media developments and trends from industry expert Andrew Hutchinson of Social Media Today.

Instagram has added another way for creators to make money, by partnering with Amazon to enable affiliate links for Amazon-listed products in Reels and feed posts.

As shown in these example images, creators can select from a range of products on Amazon to add to their posts. Clickable links to the relevant product listings on Amazon will then be added in the bottom left of a post or Reel display, allowing viewers to easily make a purchase.

All affiliate links will be connected to the creator’s account, and creators will earn a commission for any sales generated from those referrals.

Eligible creators can link their Instagram and Amazon Influencer accounts in their Professional Dashboard on IG in order to facilitate product linking.

As per an Instagram press release sent to SMT: “Once connected, Instagram automatically includes their affiliate links when they add Amazon products to content, with product links taking viewers directly to the Amazon Shopping App.”

Instagram also said creators will be able to add up to 30 products per Reel, or 20 per feed post. This should provide a heap of linking potential for affiliate opportunities.

Creators can also see the commission rate for each item, so they’ll know how much they’ll get from any resulting link clicks.

This is another way for Instagram to provide monetization pathways for its creators, while also giving Amazon another means to promote its products via creator endorsement.

Amazon has been steadily expanding its product display opportunities, with the shopping giant also inking similar affiliate partnerships with YouTube and Pinterest this year.

Though, interestingly, Amazon asked to be removed from the list of platforms that will work with Meta’s Muse agent Amazon said that was because the Muse system was in violation of Amazon’s automated shopping tool restrictions.

A partnership with Instagram, then, seems like an interesting development, which could also lead to Amazon eventually working with Meta’s other platforms more openly.

#7400. Meta launches shopping platform in Brazil

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Achados combines product listings from across the company’s apps into a unified display.

Meta has launched yet another experimental app, with the company’s new shopping-focused platform, called Achados, now available to users in Brazil.

Achados is a marketplace app that combines product listings from across Meta’s other platforms into a single, unified display of what’s available for purchase.

As per the app’s description in the Google Play store: “One feed for what people near you are selling: listings from Marketplace, posts from Facebook Groups, and items sellers have shared from Instagram, including listings you won’t find in a Marketplace search.”

As shown in the third image above, the app also includes a price comparison element, which shows how each listing matches up against similar products available in Meta’s apps.

Achados also enables potential buyers to view seller profiles on Facebook and IG, and includes customer reviews and sales histories.

According to reporting from El Fondo, Achados launched in Brazil on Tuesday, and is only available on Android at this stage.

Meta has been on an app spree of late, with the company launching six new apps this year alone, all focused on different elements.

Meta is also reportedly working on a prediction markets app, as another way to potentially expand its suite of tools.

Meta has always experimented with new apps, and most of them end up seeing limited engagement, with Meta mainly using them as a learning experience for features in its main app instead.

But the evolution of generative AI has enabled Meta to pump out app experiments more quickly, which lets the company try out more things and capitalize on common behaviors and opportunities as identified by its internal developers.

In this context, the focus on selling is noteworthy. Achados essentially aims to build on Meta’s broader shopping potential by merging all of the various product listings across its apps into a single feed.

That could provide a range of expanded promotional opportunities, while also potentially driving more shopping behavior in Meta’s other apps.

Also worth noting: Meta recently expanded its Creator Fast Track program to creators in Brazil. The combined announcements could suggest that Meta is looking to capitalize on rising opportunities in the country.

#7397. Walmart mulls role advertising will play in an agentic shopping world

Momentum for the retailer’s ad business remained strong in Q3, strengthened by assets like Vizio and an expansion of its e-commerce marketplace.

Walmart’s U.S. advertising revenue grew 37% year over year in the three-month period ended Oct. 31, bolstered by strengths in e-commerce and assets like Vizio and Vibe.co. Walmart Connect, which makes up the lion’s share of Walmart’s U.S. ad segment, was up 36% in Q3, excluding the impact of Vizio and Vibe.co, according to an earnings statement.

Vizio sold TV hardware at a steady clip, with unit volume up nearly 150% YoY, which broadens the audience Walmart advertisers can reach through the platform’s operating system, Chief Growth Officer Seth Dallaire said. The Vizio and Vibe.co acquisitions are part of Walmart’s strategy to shore up a foothold in connected TV, a premium video channel that diversifies its retail media footprint beyond onsite formats like sponsored search and display. Vibe.co, a self-service TV advertising platform, was recently integrated into Vizio and Walmart’s larger CTV offering following the acquisition’s close in August.

“We're now able to deliver capability for sales attribution through those Vizio television sets in ways that we have never been able to do before,” Dallaire said of the deal on a call discussing the retailer’s fiscal 2027 Q3. “We have a lot of room to run as we continue to sell more TVs.”

E-commerce grew 23% in Q3, with the addition of new merchants to Walmart’s online marketplace providing tailwinds for Connect. Stripping out advertising, Walmart’s e-commerce business is close to breaking even, CFO John Rainey said on the earnings call. Walmart raised its outlook for the year around the report, expecting sales growth between 4.9% to 5.2% compared to a prior forecast of 4% to 5% growth.

Executives were asked about the impact agentic artificial intelligence could have on retail media. Consumer-facing agents like Meta’s Muse can seek out deals, make product comparisons and complete transactions on the user’s behalf, which disintermediates the role ads typically play on the shopping journey.

Consumer curiosity about new products and brands hasn’t changed even as AI alters how they explore their options, per Dallaire, who nodded to the potential for Walmart to experiment with advertising in an agentic world. Walmart has its own conversational assistant, Sparky, which has seen usage in the U.S. grow 129% this year, the retailer reported. Sparky launched in June 2025.

“Conversational commerce and the way that people are creating queries and prompts in these agents is quite different than what we have seen historically,” said Dallaire. “But we are learning about those prompts and how to serve customers and allow marketers and brands to get their products in front of customers.”

Walmart is looking at agentic capabilities through two lenses as it relates to Vizio and Vibe.Co, Dallaire explained in response to an analyst follow-up question. One is the consumer-facing element, such as evolving how content is discovered and served via Vizio. Vibe.Co meanwhile is using agentic technology on the back end to draw in more small- and medium-sized advertisers that don’t have the budgets for high gloss TV campaigns.

#7393. Coca-Cola pivots from divisive AI-generated ads for holiday campaign

“Enjoy Joy” spots are part of a seasonal push that includes digital and social content, experiential activations and packaging.

Coca-Cola is going back to basics with the recently launched “Enjoy Joy” spots, showcasing moments of connection and togetherness in a decidedly traditional way. The creative direction comes after the brand ran ads during the last two years made with generative AI that — despite exceeding internal benchmarks — proved divisive with consumers. The new ads were directed by Paul Dektor and produced by Mayan Productions and arrive as holiday marketers double down on human-made creative.

The “Enjoy Joy” spots, including versions tailored to the Hispanic market, are joined by a handful of extensions across channels. Coca-Cola will run creator-led content across Meta, TikTok, Pinterest and YouTube, along with a “Grandma's Holiday Table” social series that focuses on the “abuelas, nonnas, nanas, aunties and tías” who bring holiday traditions to life. The campaign will also span streaming, audio, cinema and out-of-home channels and includes an Amazon media partnership featuring creator and commerce integrations.

“There's something special about the role Coca‑Cola plays during the holidays. People look forward to seeing how we'll show up each year, whether it's through the traditions they know and love or something entirely unexpected,” said Stacy Jackson, vice president for the Coca‑Cola Trademark, in a statement. “It's about finding new ways to make Coca‑Cola part of the season while staying true to what people have always loved about the brand.”

The brand’s long-running holiday caravan tour kicked off Nov. 5 in Nashville and will make more than 270 stops nationwide, including New York City's Times Square on Nov. 24. Each stop includes activities like photos with Santa Claus and Coke’s polar bear mascot, a gifting cabin and a beverage station. The caravan also ties into the brand’s partnership with the USO, giving consumers an opportunity to share messages with those who serve in the military.

During the holiday season, Coca‑Cola, Coca‑Cola Zero Sugar and Diet Coke will offer limited-edition packaging featuring Santa Claus, who also makes a brief appearance in the ad creative. Coca‑Cola Holiday Creamy Vanilla and its zero-sugar variant will also return to shelves for a limited time.

Coca-Cola grew net revenues by 7% to $13.4 billion in Q3 2026, beating Wall Street estimates.. The earnings were released days after the company announced the completion of its global marketing operating system review, selecting Omnicom as its media partner in North America. WPP Open X will continue to serve as Coca-Cola’s global creative partner, while Omnicom will unite media, data, CRM, loyalty and creators in a connected ecosystem in North America. The new structure will be implemented in phases beginning Dec. 1.

#7391. WTF is a unified media platform?

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

This article is a WTF explainer, in which we break down media and marketing’s most confusing terms. More from the series →

Editor’s note: These technologies are ill-defined, please share feedback to “editorial@digiday.com” to talk more.

Ad tech has never been short on acronyms. Its latest crop includes: unified advertising platforms (UAPs), unified media platforms (UMPs), and unified buying platforms (UBPs).

This kind of language has added a new layer of confusion to the space, and ad executives across adland have been saying, “WTF…?” Digiday is here to help by trying to bring some clarity to the matter.

A simple way to approach the question is to think of such terms — UAP, UBP and UMP — as overlapping labels for technology that promises to make advertising easier to buy, manage and optimize across an increasingly fragmented media landscape.

Of course, AI is central to the pitch. Advocates of such technologies maintain that instead of separately operating campaigns across Google, Meta, Amazon and the open web, advertisers could potentially use one system to determine where money goes, then execute those decisions through the relevant platforms.

Some providers propose bringing buying and selling infrastructure together. Others want to sit above existing systems and coordinate them. This is an important distinction to keep in mind, as connective software is not the same as controlling how an advertiser spends its next dollar.

At its simplest, a UMP coordinates planning, buying, optimization, and measurement across multiple media channels. Its remit could include programmatic display, CTV, retail media, search, social, and direct publisher relationships.

According to most sources, the idea is not necessarily to replace every existing media trading infrastructure, i.e., demand-side and supply-side platforms, etc., but to make them work together more efficiently.

That is distinct from the traditional remit of a DSP, many of which have spent years expanding the inventory and formats available through their own buying interfaces. A unified platform, by contrast, promises to operate across buying environments that no single DSP controls.

Jay Friedman, co-founder of CartographAI, told Digiday that the emerging proposition is about budget fluidity, not simply consolidating DSPs and SSPs. “We define a UMP as a platform that executes buys across all primary digital channels (search, social, programmatic, and direct). This may or may not include agents,” Friedman told Digiday. “Traditionally, this has been the domain of agency order management systems,” he said, noting how several new entrants are hoping to challenge these players.

The terms overlap, but they differ in what they promise to unify. The chart below traces that range, from handling an individual transaction to coordinating campaigns across channels and deciding where budgets go.

Keith Weisberg of Unplugged Collective told Digiday UBPs would initially come to market as a repackaging of existing tools. He said the deeper shift would come when buyers could bring their data to the places ads run and delegate spending decisions to AI agents.

“I’d treat that as a net-new product, not a DSP feature,” he said. “However, we’re all plugged into DSPs, and even if it’s a new product, it’ll roll out via our current commercial relationships, because that trust (and legal contracting) took years.”

Ana Milicevic, co-founder of Sparrow Advisers, described the labels as directional distinctions rather than settled product categories. She added, “It’s not all just a collection of black boxes. Then that’s a net new layer in the stack.” The key distinction is whether a platform merely pushes buttons in existing interfaces or makes independent decisions about where budgets go.

Ciarán O’Kane, general partner at First Party Capital, was more skeptical about novelty. In wording expressly cleared for attribution, he characterized UMPs and UBPs as “meta DSPs” that act as workflow layers on top of existing systems by plugging into APIs. He also said many emerging players in the space that use this terminology are revisiting approaches the industry has tried before.

Media-buying teams have long struggled with the fragmentation of conducting ad campaigns using different buying interfaces, datasets, and measurement methods. AI can make the operational work of combining and interpreting those inputs less expensive, but it does not automatically create comparable performance data or independent buying authority.

“The frustration is very, very old. What’s new now is that AI is letting us operationally skip through some hurdles that were previously insurmountable,” Milicevic told Digiday. Tasks that once demanded a substantial analytics stack and specialist labor can increasingly be assisted by repeatable prompts and automated processes.

The commercial question is whether cheaper orchestration creates an entirely new buyer or simply a more convenient way to operate existing platforms. For advertisers, that distinction determines whether the new technology saves money, reallocates money, or merely adds another vendor to pay.

In an Oct. 8 blog post, titled “universal buying platform,” Apostrophe co-founders Brian O’Kelley and Anne Coghlan outlined a broader buying layer intended to connect intent, execution, and measurement across advertising environments, citing the recent launch of Amazon Ads Agent as an example of this trend.

Their argument is a public proposal, not evidence that a single independent platform already performs every part of the workflow at scale — O’Kelley did not respond to Digiday’s request for comment by press time.

Other companies are approaching the problem from different directions, with Olyzon — an entity that used the term “agentic orchestration platform” in its recent CTV launch material — serving as one example.

Jules Minvielle, co-founder and CEO of Olyzon, told Digiday the company does not own inventory and uses agents to coordinate buying through existing platforms, with advertiser-controlled approvals. “A DSP is one place where you can buy. Orchestration is all the work around the buy: building the plan, deciding where each dollar goes, activating across every platform, watching delivery, measuring, and feeding what worked into the next plan,” he wrote in an emailed statement, adding that uneven API access and inconsistent measurement remain substantial obstacles.

Ravi Patel, co-founder and CEO of SWYM.ai, drew a distinction between a common interface and control over the budget. “It can manage the ‘what’ and the ‘how much.’ It can’t manage the ‘which impression.’ That distinction is the whole story,” he explained, adding that campaign setup and pacing across platforms are already possible through APIs. However, impression-level optimization within the major walled gardens remains in their own systems.

Lucas Cridland, CEO of Basin Media, questioned whether agents are changing the underlying buying process. “At the moment, many environments are agentically operating in the same supply chain flow. They’re just doing it with agents buying; they’re not radically changing the way the process works. And that’s where the real edge exists.”

Not necessarily. Many of the new propositions depend on DSPs continuing to execute transactions, access inventory, and optimize within their own environments. The change may be who decides which platform gets the advertiser’s budget in the first place.

Milicevic maintained that “value always accrues to whoever is closest to the decision or outright owns the decision.”

Ergo, if DSPs cannot move upstream into genuine cross-channel decision-making, they risk becoming “dumb pipes,” i.e., a commoditized, essential infrastructure whose underlying value is less visible to buyers.

Incumbent DSPs, however, have established advertiser relationships, bidding technology, data integrations and scale. They could develop their own orchestration capabilities. A new intermediary would need to show that its independence produces better decisions, rather than simply adding another fee, according to several sources consulted for this piece.

Agencies already coordinate campaigns across channels, negotiate buying arrangements and manage the systems needed to execute them. Friedman, a former long-time agency-lead, argued that unified platforms could challenge agency order-management systems while bringing neglected channels, including creator-led media, into a common workflow.

Such a scenario would not eliminate strategic planning, creative development, or oversight, but it could change how much manual campaign administration agencies perform, where they develop proprietary expertise, and which services clients are willing to pay for, with AI at the center of many contractual renegotiations between advertisers and media agencies.

Such a scenario would not eliminate strategic planning, creative development, or oversight, but it could change how much manual campaign administration agencies perform, where they develop proprietary expertise, and which services clients are willing to pay for, with AI at the center of many contractual renegotiations between advertisers and media agencies.

The biggest obstacle may be the very platforms that independent systems are supposed to coordinate. Google, Meta, and Amazon control their own buying tools, data, and optimization systems. Access through an API does not necessarily provide the granular information or authority required to compare results and independently move budgets.

Trust is also an issue. A unified interface can display reports from multiple platforms without proving that the outcomes are comparable. Attribution windows, campaign objectives, and self-reported measurement differ across channels, meaning an independent buying layer needs a credible basis for deciding that one dollar is better spent in one environment than another.

Friedman was skeptical that interoperability would resolve the underlying problems, and highlighted how Google and Meta are unlikely to make their systems interoperable. He further questioned whether agencies could build the context and routing infrastructure needed to translate employees’ instructions into accurate buying decisions, even if every platform supported the Model Context Protocol. “Interoperability won’t solve anything,” he told Digiday.

Greg MacDonald, founder of Chelsea Strategies, said addressing media buying’s growing complexity could reduce spending on its mechanics and, over time, potentially improve advertiser outcomes. However, he remained cautious about how far unified platforms could go. “I’m skeptical that the walled gardens will give buyers more control than they have today,” he told Digiday, pointing to persistent difficulties with frequency capping across programmatic formats, let alone disconnected marketplaces.

“But you don’t have to solve every problem, and certainly not on day one to be useful,” MacDonald added. He compared the opportunity with programmatic guaranteed buying, which made the insertion-order process more efficient without fundamentally changing direct media buying.

Meanwhile, Milicevic said the more plausible near-term customer may be a multichannel advertiser between the largest global brands and small businesses that largely rely on a single platform. She also stressed the need for independent validation of results, rather than taking each platform’s own reporting at face value.

AI agents could help automate campaign setup, inventory discovery, negotiation, execution, and reporting. That makes agentic advertising relevant to the unified-platform debate, but not synonymous with it: automating tasks is different from making accountable, cross-channel budget decisions.

Unplugged Collective maps seven ways AI could change advertising (see graphic below). Per Weisberg, two are central to the unified buying debate: how agents decide where to spend and how they carry out those decisions across platforms.

Industry initiatives such as AdCP and IAB Tech Lab’s agentic work aim to establish ways for systems to communicate and execute workflows. Technical standards could reduce integration costs, but they cannot by themselves force walled gardens to disclose comparable data or surrender commercial control.

Seasoned industry observers have seen enough buzz phrases come and go to feel confident enough to make tongue-in-cheek observations when a new acronym becomes de rigueur among the industry’s chattering classes.

“I saw ‘UMP’ and I thought: ad network,” quipped Terence Kawaja, CEO, LUMA Partners, a prominent investment bank in the space, speaking on stage at this year’s Prebid Summit – channeling the industry adage, “everything is an ad network.”

Meanwhile, one prominent industry source, who requested anonymity to preserve industry relationships, dismissed the latest acronyms as another serving of “word salad.” However, the barbs, playful or otherwise, raise a palpable industry debate: As AI makes it cheaper to run campaigns across platforms, who gets to decide where the money goes?

The clearest test is whether advertisers entrust meaningful cross-channel allocation to an independent system — and can demonstrate that the decisions improve outcomes. Smaller-scale automation and campaign management do not, on their own, prove that a new market category has emerged.

Milicevic identified three indicators: real advertiser budgets moving under independent allocation, credible measurement outside any single walled garden, and changes in how marketing organizations structure teams. “If it’s truly structural, it’ll show up in the PNL, and if it’s slideware and fluff, then that’ll make its way into award entries and kind of the PR circuit,” she said.

For now, the most useful distinction is between controlling the infrastructure through which ads are purchased and controlling the decision about where the money goes. The latter is the more ambitious promise. Whether anyone can deliver it independently, at scale, and at a cost advertisers will accept remains unresolved.

After 45 years of negotiating, the chairman of Publicis Media Exchange (PMX) U.S., is stepping down from a full-time role this year, although he plans to continue to consult with the holdco.

Mediaplus and Publicis clients, including Banco Santander, have paused TikTok spending in wake of investigations into abuse images circulating on the video platform.

Madison and Wall estimates creator marketing growing at double the pace of the rest of advertising, driven on by creator-led production investments.
